# Frontend unit-testing mutation triage

## Purpose and register status

This is the active mutation triage register. The current observation index and behavior decisions are maintained under the rules in this opening section. Older run records are retained for reference; the active rules here govern updates.

**Current state:** latest full report `FULL-2026-11-06-priority1-4-final1` (2026-11-06): 531 survivors, 57 NoCoverage and 20 timeouts. All 608 non-killed observations are indexed below and now linked to individually checked decisions; no observation mapping remains unresolved. T2-026 records the final grouped investigation for the last 16 observations. Previous focused-run counts and decisions remain historical evidence, not refreshed full-run results. This triage does not refresh or claim a green full-run baseline.

## Active structure and rules

1. **Behavior decisions:** retain the existing UTM/FULL IDs, rationale, evidence, scope, assumptions and revisit triggers in the copied decision register. One decision can explain multiple mutations. Preserve distinctions between equivalent, accepted as irrelevant within stated scope, runner limitation, fix required and awaiting investigation; do not equate an observed Stryker status with a decision.
2. **Run observations:** the **Current full-run observation index** contains one row for every non-killed mutation in the latest full report, with a full-run-qualified reference, file, complete location, mutator, replacement, raw status and decision mapping. A reference `run label / mutant ID` is unique only inside that report. Source hashes identify the exact report-embedded source. Do not add scoped-run mutant IDs to this full-run index; record their results in the run record and link them as newer evidence to the exact affected decision/observation.
3. **Mapping:** replace `Unmapped` with one applicable decision ID only after checking the precise original expression, replacement, surrounding code, run scope and decision evidence. If a decision has changed or does not cover the mutation, add a specific decision or leave it awaiting investigation. An old green source row does not automatically accept all current observations. Decisions and observations are related records; their counts must not be summed together.
4. **Across runs:** use file, original expression, operator, replacement and source context to propose candidates. These fields can collide; a line can also move or code can change. Accept an automatic match only where uniqueness and unchanged relevant assumptions are established; otherwise record an ambiguous or changed case for human review. A hash or mutant ID alone does not prove equivalence. Source and test revisions, static mutation behavior, tool version and affected caller assumptions also matter.
5. **Run scope:** a full run reports its complete configured scope. A localized run only refreshes its stated source/test scope; absence outside that scope says nothing about whether a mutation was killed or removed. Incremental reports can contain reused results: preserve the run configuration and distinguish reused results from freshly executed ones where the report allows. Keep raw reports and tested revision available before they are overwritten.
6. **Completeness:** reconcile each report status and unique run-qualified ID to exactly one evidenced decision or an explicitly unresolved mapping; verify totals against the raw report. A timeout requires evidence before being accepted as detected behavior. NoCoverage requires a reachability decision or coverage work. Keep the full and incremental run records distinct.
7. **Batch work by source file before Stryker:** collect all pending investigations, required source/test fixes and missing assertions for the same source file. Finish that file's related changes together, run its focused unit spec or specs, then run one forced localized Stryker scope for that source file with all relevant specs. Do not run Stryker once per survivor, test, or individual fix. If the run exposes another gap in the same file, batch the follow-up fixes before rerunning that file. If multiple file groups are ready together, use one invocation for them when the selected specs cover each source and the resulting observations remain attributable; otherwise run one invocation per file group. Keep Stryker jobs serial because they share report output.

### Procedure for every mutation run

1. Before running, record the local start time and timezone, Git revision, staged and unstaged changes, exact command, source/test filters, configuration and tool version. Record whether the run is forced or incremental. If a field is unavailable, say so rather than reconstructing it from a later checkout.
2. Run mutation jobs serially. Inspect the JSON from the completed run; it is the required source for scope, totals, statuses and mutant details. Preserve every completed full-run JSON/HTML and its tested revision before replacing the active full-run snapshot. For incremental/scoped reports, preserve JSON/HTML before overwrite only when a current decision depends on their detailed evidence; otherwise the run record and decision rationale are sufficient. Record artifact paths and SHA-256 checksums. A workspace-local ignored archive is not a version-controlled backup. Inspecting an existing tar archive is not a routine step: use one only to recover a cited prior report that is otherwise unavailable or to verify a decision that depends on its archived details.
3. Reconcile the raw report: record completion time, scope, status totals and report label. For each non-killed observation record `run label / mutant ID`, source path, source hash, complete location, mutator, replacement and status. Preserve the exact original expression in the archived report-embedded source identified by that hash and location; it can be reconstructed from the report without duplicating it in the table. Keep timeout details and coverage/test details where available. Identify cached versus freshly executed results only when the evidence supports it; otherwise mark them unknown.
4. Match observations to behavior decisions using the exact expression and its context, operator and replacement, then verify the decision's behavior, evidence, tested scope, callers, tests and assumptions. IDs and coordinates are run-local; repeated expressions and three-line contexts can collide. A proposed match is unresolved if it is ambiguous, the code or tests changed materially, or the evidence does not support the decision. Check moved code, changed operator/replacement, static mutants and timeout results explicitly. Never accept a match solely from a count, ID, source hash or matching line.
5. Update the tables according to run type; never substitute a scoped result for the full baseline.
   - **Full run — update these sections:** preserve the previous full JSON/HTML where needed and the old index before replacing the active snapshot. Read the complete non-killed set and totals from the new JSON, then compare it to the previous index by source hash, exact location/expression, mutator, replacement and relevant source/test/caller assumptions. Rebuild **Current full-run observation index** from every non-killed observation in the new report. For unique exact matches whose relevant code, tests and assumptions are unchanged, carry forward the checked decision/rationale and update the raw status, full-run reference and action status; do not repeat the investigation. Investigate only new/changed observations, ambiguous matches or observations whose relevant assumptions changed. Do not carry a decision using counts or mutant IDs alone. Remove newly killed observations from the active non-killed index while retaining their prior evidence in run history. Update **New behavior decisions during reconciliation** or the behavior-decision register only for a genuinely new behavior or changed conclusion. Update **Current state** with the new full-run label/date and raw totals after reconciliation. Append the run metadata and result to **Referenced mutation testing runs record**. Add an entry to **Preserved focused evidence** only if the retained artifact supports a current decision and must remain inspectable. Leave historical entries intact.
   - **Incremental or other scoped run — update these sections:** append a separate entry in **Referenced mutation testing runs record** with the exact source/test filters and result. Match only observations in that scope to the current full index and existing decisions using the across-run rule. For an exact full-index match, keep its full-run **Stryker status** unchanged, but update **Action status**, decision mapping/evidence reference, and revisit trigger only if the scoped evidence changes that disposition. Add/split a behavior-decision row only for a genuinely new behavior or changed conclusion. Keep **Current full-run observation index** and **Current state** raw full-run totals unchanged. Record scoped-only observations in the scoped run record or the relevant decision evidence; never add them as full-run observations. Do not treat omitted files/mutations as killed. Add an entry to **Preserved focused evidence** only when retaining that report is necessary to support an ongoing decision after generated reports are overwritten. Leave unrelated rows and historical entries untouched.
6. Preserve each prior decision's reason, evidence, scope, assumptions and revisit triggers when updating or superseding it. Revisit it when relevant source, callers, tests or assumptions change. List every unresolved observation precisely and do not claim a green triage until all remaining non-killed observations are killed or individually supported by applicable evidence. A completed triage does not mean the raw full report contains no survivors, NoCoverage or timeouts.

### How to inspect reports and update the register

1. **Start with the report from the run being recorded.** Read its JSON, not just the console summary or HTML. Confirm `framework`, `config` scope, configured test files, each source's embedded `source`, and all `files[*].mutants[*]`. Check the status totals against the completed run. For every non-killed mutation in the relevant scope, inspect its run-local ID, location, mutator, replacement, `status`, `coveredBy`, `killedBy` and test names in `testFiles`; inspect the embedded source at the reported location to recover the original expression. Use HTML to inspect a mutation in readable context or investigate unexpected results. JSON determines the recorded status.
2. **When a cited older report is available only in a tar:** list its contents, extract it to a temporary directory, and verify extracted file hashes against the checksums in this document/run record before relying on it:

   ```sh
   tar -tzf path/to/evidence.tar.gz
   shasum -a 256 path/to/evidence.tar.gz
   review_dir="$(mktemp -d)"
   tar -xzf path/to/evidence.tar.gz -C "$review_dir"
   find "$review_dir" -type f -print
   shasum -a 256 path/to/extracted/report.json path/to/extracted/report.html
   ```

   Then inspect the extracted JSON/HTML as in step 1. Do not extract into the generated reports directory unless restoring files for a tool that requires those exact paths.
3. **Compare before editing.** For a full run, compare its complete non-killed set to the previous full index. For an incremental run, compare only the exact source/test scope to the full index and relevant decision rows. Use source hash plus exact expression/location, mutator, replacement, test scope and unchanged assumptions to confirm a match. List test names from `coveredBy` when coverage evidence explains why a mutation was or was not exercised. Ambiguous matches remain unresolved until reviewed.
4. **Apply the update steps in the Full run or Incremental/scoped run bullets above.** The full index stores raw observations from the latest full run only. Scoped outcomes update the matching row's action/decision evidence but never overwrite its raw full-run status. Update run records and summary totals only for the run type those sections describe.

Example JSON inspection for one report (prints per-file status totals and details for non-killed mutations):

```sh
python3 - path/to/stryker-incremental.json <<'PY'
import collections
import hashlib
import json
import sys

report = json.load(open(sys.argv[1], encoding="utf-8"))
print("framework:", report.get("framework", {}).get("name"), report.get("framework", {}).get("version"))
print("configured source filters:", report.get("config", {}).get("mutate"))
print("reported test files:", list(report.get("testFiles", {})))
tests = {
    test["id"]: test["name"]
    for file in report.get("testFiles", {}).values()
    for test in file.get("tests", [])
}
totals = collections.Counter()
for path, source_file in report.get("files", {}).items():
    mutants = source_file.get("mutants", [])
    source_hash = hashlib.sha256(source_file.get("source", "").encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    counts = collections.Counter(m.get("status") for m in mutants)
    totals.update(counts)
    print(path, "source SHA-256:", source_hash, dict(counts))
    for mutant in mutants:
        if mutant.get("status") == "Killed":
            continue
        start = mutant.get("location", {}).get("start", {})
        test_names = [tests.get(test_id, test_id) for test_id in mutant.get("coveredBy", [])]
        lines = source_file.get("source", "").splitlines()
        line_number = start.get("line", 0)
        context = "\n".join(f"{number + 1}: {lines[number]}" for number in range(max(0, line_number - 2), min(len(lines), line_number + 1)))
        print({key: mutant.get(key) for key in ("id", "mutatorName", "replacement", "status", "coveredBy", "killedBy")}, "at", start, "tests", test_names)
        print(context)
print("total statuses:", dict(totals))
PY
```

### Preserved focused evidence for mapped observations

Each linked focused report has the same embedded source and one exact location/operator/replacement match for its referenced current observation. Prior IDs are local to that report. This confirms identity, not that a changed caller or test cannot invalidate a rationale; the decision's stated assumptions and revisit conditions remain in force. The individual files under `TTLeaguePlayersApp.FrontEnd/reports/` are ignored, mutable workspace copies and can be overwritten by a later run. The tar packages preserve the raw JSON and HTML beyond that workspace lifecycle: JSON is the machine-readable source for statuses, locations, source hashes and coverage/killed-by metadata; HTML is the inspectable report view. The main [evidence package](../../../mutation-evidence/frontend-mutation-evidence-2026-11-06.tar.gz) contains the full report and six focused report pairs (14 files; SHA-256 `47254581d46df05a314728b845ee70c498fad7d7f981774e0349fafb73fad209`); the separate [4760 recheck package](../../../mutation-evidence/promote-my-tournaments-4760-recheck-2026-11-06.tar.gz) preserves that follow-up run. The packages are untracked until the user chooses to commit or otherwise preserve them. Future maintainers/agents inspect the JSON when verifying a table mapping, reconciling a later full run, or revisiting a decision; a human reviewer can inspect the HTML for readable mutation context. They need not unpack every package for every run: inspect only when a current decision depends on that artifact, a mapping is ambiguous, or relevant code/tests/assumptions change. Extract into a temporary directory for review; restore ignored workspace copies only when a tool needs them.

### Matching-rule checks

- The archived full report contains two real candidates in `MainMenu.tsx`, IDs 822 and 830: original expression, operator, replacement and three surrounding lines coincide. They belong to different menu entries. Such a collision cannot be assigned automatically; both entries were separately inspected before T2-003 was applied.
- If a unique expression moves, locate its current caller and check changed source and tests before accepting a prior decision; a changed operator, replacement or behavior contract requires a new or unresolved decision. A matching source hash alone does not prove the test revision or caller behavior.
- A static mutant or timeout retains its reported status; accept a runner or detection explanation only with supporting evidence. If an archived report or its test scope is missing, record the limit rather than assuming a replay occurred.
- For a scoped report, an absent mutation outside the configured source/test filters is not a kill; the last full-run totals remain unchanged. An incremental result may be reused, so mark freshness unknown unless supported by run evidence.
## Historical behavior decisions

This table preserves earlier decisions, statuses, dates, counts, reasons, next actions and evidence references. Historical counts and broad source summaries overlap and must not be added to current observations. Earlier acceptances are reconsidered when the current observation index identifies a changed assumption or an unresolved finding.

| UTM-001 | `service/frontend-diagnostics/FrontendEventQueue.ts` | 🟢 | Accepted: bounded equivalence and observability | 9 / 0 / 0 | 2026-11-02 | Final targeted run: 151 killed and 9 survived. Mutant 11's primitive entry guard is redundant because later field checks reject primitives. Envelope guard mutants 43, 44, 45, 46 and 48 reach later validation or the catch for malformed persisted values; with normal storage they yield the same empty queue. When storage operations fail transiently, the catch can attempt `removeItem` a different number of times; cleanup is best effort: a transient failure can leave invalid data until a later trigger rather than normalize it on this pass. This difference is accepted for that failure mode, not claimed as strict equivalence. Mutant 88 removes the undefined status guard, redundant with numeric status comparisons; 112 evaluates `Date.now() - undefined` as NaN, making the cooldown comparison false; 151 substitutes `{}` for `{ success: false }`, both falsy at the only success check. UTM-014 retains the historical timeout decisions. | Retain malformed-input, storage-failure and drain lifecycle assertions; revisit if storage cleanup attempts or these contracts become observable requirements. | INC-2026-11-02-diagnostics-final |

STATUS_LABEL/STATUS_PILL_COLOUR/STATUS_ORDER ObjectLiteral and StringLiteral mutations (9): the React Compiler (babel-plugin-react-compiler) inlines module-level constants into the compiled component; Stryker mutates the source declaration but the compiled output is unaffected, confirmed by "Ran all tests for this mutant" with no failures on every STATUS constant mutant. State-init booleans (2): `isLoading=true` and `hasClubTeamsError=false` are overwritten by `setIsLoading(true)` and `setHasClubTeamsError(false)` on the first render cycle before any assertion. `listVersion` counter direction (1): `++` vs `--` both produce a unique token per call; the equality check `version === listVersion` detects staleness either way. `isCurrent()` guards in finally and empty-teams branch (2): `if (true)` mutations call `setIsLoading(false)` or `setTeams([])` on a re-rendered component; React 18 silently ignores state updates on unmounted components, and the empty-teams `isCurrent()` guard is only reachable when `loadStatuses` is called synchronously within the `readWithUpdates` callback, making the race it guards against unreachable in practice. `if (!cancelled) -> if (true)` in catch (1): same React 18 no-op reasoning for state updates after unmount. Effect cleanup variants (3): `cancelled = true -> false`, `-> {}`, and deps `-> []`; the re-render tests cover the cancellation contract and React 18 makes the state-update side effects unobservable after unmount. console.error strings (2): observability-only strings. Separator else-branch `'' -> "Stryker was here!"` (1): the else branch renders when the ternary is false so the replacement string is never in the DOM. | Retain all regression tests; reevaluate if React Compiler is removed, React version changes, or the effect/cleanup contract changes. | INC-2026-11-02-club-teams-team-players |

Envelope guard mutants 43, 44, 45, 46 and 48 reach later validation or the catch for malformed persisted values; with normal storage they yield the same empty queue. When storage operations fail transiently, the catch can attempt `removeItem` a different number of times; cleanup is best effort: a transient failure can leave invalid data until a later trigger rather than normalize it on this pass. This difference is accepted for that failure mode, not claimed as strict equivalence. Mutant 88 removes the undefined status guard, redundant with numeric status comparisons; 112 evaluates `Date.now() - undefined` as NaN, making the cooldown comparison false; 151 substitutes `{}` for `{ success: false }`, both falsy at the only success check. STATUS_LABEL/STATUS_PILL_COLOUR/STATUS_ORDER ObjectLiteral and StringLiteral mutations (9): the React Compiler (babel-plugin-react-compiler) inlines module-level constants into the compiled component; Stryker mutates the source declaration but the compiled output is unaffected, confirmed by "Ran all tests for this mutant" with no failures on every STATUS constant mutant. State-init booleans (2): `isLoading=true` and `hasClubTeamsError=false` are overwritten by `setIsLoading(true)` and `setHasClubTeamsError(false)` on the first render cycle before any assertion. `listVersion` counter direction (1): `++` vs `--` both produce a unique token per call; the equality check `version === listVersion` detects staleness either way. `isCurrent()` guards in finally and empty-teams branch (2): `if (true)` mutations call `setIsLoading(false)` or `setTeams([])` on a re-rendered component; React 18 silently ignores state updates on unmounted components, and the empty-teams `isCurrent()` guard is only reachable when `loadStatuses` is called synchronously within the `readWithUpdates` callback, making the race it guards against unreachable in practice. `if (!cancelled) -> if (true)` in catch (1): same React 18 no-op reasoning for state updates after unmount. Effect cleanup variants (3): `cancelled = true -> false`, `-> {}`, and deps `-> []`; the re-render tests cover the cancellation contract and React 18 makes the state-update side effects unobservable after unmount. console.error strings (2): observability-only strings. Separator else-branch `'' -> "Stryker was here!"` (1): the else branch renders when the ternary is false so the replacement string is never in the DOM. | Retain all regression tests; reevaluate if React Compiler is removed, React version changes, or the effect/cleanup contract changes. | INC-2026-11-02-club-teams-team-players |

# Unit-testing mutation triage

## Document purpose and references

Version-controlled register of problems surfaced by frontend unit-test mutation testing
and findings investigated and determined not to require a fix. Update this register
alongside test or source changes. Human and AI reviewers use the same evidence requirements.

Commands and report destinations are documented in
[FrontEnd Verification Commands](../instructions/FrontEnd%20VerificationCommands.md#3-commands-to-run-frontend-mutation-tests).
This register is a decision record, not generated output or a Stryker configuration file.



## Current mutation testing results and triage decisions record
Earlier behavior decisions are listed in [Historical behavior decisions](#historical-behavior-decisions).

### Decision states glossary

| Action status | Decision | Meaning | Next action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 🟢 | Resolved | A fix has been verified. | Record the verification command, outcome and date. |
| 🟢 | Accepted: no fix required | Evidence establishes equivalence, an unreachable supported path, or another justified non-problem. | Preserve the reason, assumptions and reevaluation triggers. |
| ⚠️ | Needs reevaluation | Relevant evidence or assumptions have changed since the decision. | Reopen investigation before relying on the previous conclusion. |
| ⚠️ | Awaiting investigation | No supported determination yet. | Inspect the exact mutation, relevant code, tests and selected run scope. |
| 🛑 | Deferred: known gap | A real issue remains, including visual coverage debt. | Keep a concrete action or linked issue; do not count it as resolved. |
| 🛑 | Fix required | A meaningful test gap, production defect or verification problem is confirmed. | Record the specific fix and priority; implement and verify it. |


Stryker statuses (`Survived`, `NoCoverage`, `Timeout`, `RuntimeError`, `CompileError`,
`Ignored`, `Pending`, `Killed`) are observations. Decision states above are review conclusions.

A survivor does not establish a production bug; an uncovered site may reflect selected-test
scope. A timeout caused by a mutation introducing an infinite loop is detected behaviour,
whereas an infrastructure timeout needs investigation. Record which case the evidence supports.

Paths below are relative to `TTLeaguePlayersApp.FrontEnd/`. This is a compact
**referenced-runs register**, not a detailed run-history log. Retain the latest completed full
baseline and every targeted incremental run cited by a current inventory-table row. Remove a
run only after no current row cites it. This makes a table containing a full baseline plus newer
targeted decisions traceable without pretending that a targeted run refreshed the whole suite.

Each record has a stable label. Every new or updated table row must state its supporting label in
the **Evidence run** column, for example `FULL-2026-10-30` or
`INC-2026-10-30-parser`. The determination date remains the date the reviewer made the decision;
it is not a substitute for the evidence-run timestamp.

## How Tos for this document updates

### Accepted findings and suppression policy

An accepted decision can remain visible as `Survived` or `NoCoverage` in Stryker.
Recording acceptance here does not automatically suppress anything or change the score.
This register is the central record for both accepted findings and actual exclusions.

If an explicit Stryker exclusion is implemented, record the precise configuration/directive,
its scope, reason and corresponding register ID. Prefer a narrow, reasoned exclusion to a
file-wide ignore. A source directive can reference the register entry. Missing behavioural
tests, deferred work and uninvestigated timeouts/errors are not reasons to suppress findings.

Every accepted finding must state the conditions that make it harmless. Reevaluate when relevant
source, callers, tests, fixtures, contracts, runtime assumptions, dependencies, Stryker version
or test configuration changes. A change after the determination date is a reason to check
relevance, not proof of invalidity; unrelated edits need not reopen the decision. Use the
reason/evidence and Git history to identify relevant changes. Neither Git nor Stryker automatically
revalidates a Markdown decision or an exclusion comment.

No new Stryker exclusion has been added as part of creating this register.


### Maintaining the table

There is exactly **one** mutation inventory table: the table directly below
**Current mutation testing results and triage decisions record**. Never add a second inventory,
source-inventory, summary or continuation table elsewhere in this document. Add source-level
rows, exact mutation rows and total rows to that same table, using its existing header and
columns. Do not repeat its header or separator midway through the table.

Keep all triage information directly in that table: finding, observed status/counts, decision
state, determination date, evidence-run label, brief reason/evidence and next action. Record the
evidence-run label in the **Evidence run** column. Use `Pending` for the determination date until
investigation supports a decision. On reevaluation, update the date, run label and reason; Git
preserves the previous versions.

Identify findings precisely enough to revisit them: add the function, original expression,
replacement or mutator name to the File / finding cell as needed. Split a file-level row when
its mutations need different decisions. Link a test, report or issue in the reason/action cell
when useful. Separate detailed records and a per-finding template are not required.

Keep report provenance in the referenced-runs register and cite its label in each row. Do not
infer run dates from filesystem timestamps or treat mixed incremental results as a fresh full run.
For every future run, record the local date and time to the minute, tested revision (including
relevant uncommitted changes), source and test scope, completion status, result summary, and
machine-artifact status. The full JSON/HTML are baseline artifacts; incremental JSON/HTML are
mutable working artifacts and may be superseded. Archive a targeted artifact separately only when
its detailed machine evidence must remain inspectable after the next incremental run.

For a targeted incremental run, update only the rows for the exact mutated source files and
selected test scope. Keep the full-baseline rows for every other source unchanged. If the targeted
result changes a previously recorded finding, update that row's counts, decision, determination
date, reason and **Evidence run** label to the new incremental record. If it reveals a new,
distinct behaviour group, add or split that row in this same table. Do not update the full-baseline
total or claim that the full report has been refreshed: the current picture is the full baseline
overlaid by the explicitly referenced incremental rows.

A source-level row is an inventory and work-planning aid, not evidence that every mutation in
that source has the same conclusion. A broad `Fix required` row must state the concrete
follow-up and remains conservative until split into behaviour-level decisions. Never report
whole-report triage as complete merely because every source has an inventory row: completion
requires that every non-killed result is covered by an evidenced exact-mutation or precisely
defined behaviour-group decision, with no unreviewed remainder hidden by a broad row.

### Updating red rows after a fix

A code or test change alone does not resolve a red (`🛑`) finding. Run Stryker for the affected
source and relevant test scope, then update the table from that completed run:

1. For a behavior-level finding whose mutations are now all killed, update that same row to
   `🟢` / `Resolved`, set its remaining counts to zero, and record the new determination date,
   evidence-run label, test evidence and next action (normally to retain the regression tests).
   Keep the row and its ID; do not delete resolved findings.
2. For a broad source-level row, replace its survived, no-coverage and timeout counts with the
   latest outstanding counts from the targeted run. Keep it `🛑` / `Fix required` while any
   untriaged mutations remain, even if the specific behavior fixed is resolved. If the run
   establishes a distinct resolved behavior group, record that group in its own behavior-level
   row and link the broad row's evidence to it. Do not mark the entire source resolved based
   only on a partial fix or report partial targeted counts as full-baseline counts.
3. If all remaining non-killed mutations in the row have been investigated and accepted, update
   the decision state to the applicable accepted state rather than `Resolved`; preserve their
   non-killed counts and document why they are harmless. Use `Resolved` when the finding's
   mutations are killed, not merely because the implementation task is complete.
4. If the targeted run does not include the finding's exact source/test scope, do not change its
   status or counts. Record the run separately only if it is relevant to another current row.

### Document update procedure: preserve this structure

1. Read the matching JSON and confirm the actual source/test scope and completion. Identify
   findings against the current code before carrying previous decisions forward. A full baseline
   plus any newer incremental results and this table provides the picture for the revisions tested;
   the table alone does not reconstruct all mutation results.
2. After a successful run, add or update its entry in **Referenced mutation testing runs record**.
   Record a stable label, local date and time to the minute, tested revision (and relevant
   uncommitted changes), scope, completion, results and artifact status. Keep the latest full
   baseline and every incremental record cited by a current row; remove an older record only when
   no row cites it. A focused run never replaces the full baseline. Leave the last completed record
   intact after an interrupted/failed attempt; do not label that attempt a completed baseline.
3. For a full run, reconcile all affected rows. For a targeted incremental run, reconcile only the
   exact mutated source/test scope reported in that run's JSON: update those rows in place and
   leave every other full-baseline row intact. Retain IDs and all existing columns, including
   **Decision state** and **Evidence run**. Add or split rows only for new findings or different
   decisions, always in the one inventory table. For statuses without a dedicated count column,
   such as `RuntimeError` or `CompileError`, state the status and exact mutation in **File /
   finding** and explain the investigation in **Reason / evidence**. Set **Evidence run** to the
   full or incremental label that resolves to the referenced-runs register. Do not alter the full
   baseline total after a targeted run, and do not present out-of-scope historical counts as freshly
   tested.
4. Set the decision and determination date only when evidence supports it. Keep `Pending` until
   then. Put a brief explanation/test reference in **Reason / evidence** and the concrete follow-up
   in **Next action**. Reopen relevant changed findings as **Needs reevaluation**; a rerun alone does
   not renew an acceptance decision. Retain resolved and accepted rows so their conclusions remain
   visible. Update the mixed-inventory total only when its underlying row counts change.
5. Before saying that a full report has been fully triaged, reconcile its JSON totals and exact
   non-killed mutant set against the table. A targeted incremental run may only support the claim
   that its selected scope was triaged; it does not revalidate the rest of the full baseline. Every
   mutant in the claimed scope must map to one evidenced decision or precisely scoped behaviour
   group; source-level rows alone do not satisfy this check. Record any remainder explicitly as
   **Awaiting investigation** or **Fix required** rather than implying completion.
6. Check that dates, run labels, counts, decisions and references agree, then run `git diff --check`.
   Keep the existing sections, decision vocabulary and one-table shape. Do not add per-finding
   templates, separate decision-record sections, additional tables, detailed run logs, repeated
   console output or redundant reports. Git preserves prior versions; generated JSON/HTML retain
   the machine results. Changes to this agreed structure require a user request.
